El seed genera fechas con new Date(...).toISOString(), algo como 2026-08-13T15:30:00.000Z. Pero las órdenes creadas normalmente dejan que SQLite use CURRENT_TIMESTAMP, que produce un formato diferente, aproximadamente 2026-08-13 15:30:00. No es solamente duplicación. Significa que ahora hay dos formas de acceder a orders y que cualquier invariant, tenant rule, instrumentación o abstracción añadida al DAL puede ser omitida por metrics. No documenta respuestas, errores, rangos, semántica de fechas, límites ni suficientes contratos. architecture.md dice que existe un “Single shared db instance” mientras que metrics.ts crea otra conexión. Es decir, hay contradicciones entre documentación y comportamiento real.\n\nHay más cosas que podrían considerarse issues, pero no todas merecen seis horas. El frontend no maneja errores HTTP correctamente; api() simplemente hace .json() aunque el response sea 4xx/5xx. Las requests de refresh() se hacen secuencialmente y pueden existir carreras si cambias rápido de merchant. Un refresh puede incluso empezar con un merchant y continuar haciendo requests después de que cambie select.value, lo que abre la posibilidad de mostrar estado mezclado entre merchants. El middleware acepta cualquier X-Merchant-Id sin validar existencia; crear una orden para un merchant inexistente termina delegando el fallo al foreign key. No hay constraints de DB para type, status o amounts. No hay prácticamente observabilidad. Incluso dentro de su modelo simplificado, una request identificada como merchant A nunca debería recuperar los recursos de merchant B.\n\nLa distinción es importante: no confundir una simplificación deliberada con el bug de aislamiento que existe detrás de esa simplificación.\n\nSobre las tres features\n\nSin elegir todavía ninguna: tienen perfiles de riesgo muy diferentes.\n\nCSV export parece acotada a primera vista, pero te obliga a decidir escaping, formatos, auth, fecha/moneda y qué hacer con datasets grandes.\n\nWebhooks es claramente la de mayor superficie arquitectónica. No hay análisis técnico.\n\nWrite this yourself, without AI assistance...\nEs una advertencia, no se responde. Te está diciendo que la redacción final debe ser tuya. Yo puedo ayudarte a entender las preguntas y comprobar hechos, pero no redactarte las respuestas.\n\nTwo pages max. Specifics over generalities.\nMáximo dos páginas. Prefieren cosas concretas como “este método no filtraba por merchant” a frases generales como “mejoré la seguridad”.\n\nConfidence and disagreement are part of the score — own both.\nQuieren que seas sincero con tu nivel de seguridad y que muestres cuándo no aceptaste una sugerencia de IA. No esperan que todo sea 10/10.\n\n## Authorship declaration\nAquí declaras cómo se escribió este documento. Como ahora estás usando IA para entender el template y comprobar qué significa cada apartado, pero no para que escriba tus respuestas, debes reflejar de forma honesta ese uso limitado. No conviene afirmar “sin ninguna asistencia de IA” si estás usando esta conversación para orientarte. Tú debes formular la declaración final.\n\n## Issues addressed\nAquí empiezan los problemas que decidiste corregir. Necesitas al menos los tres que quieras presentar como tus issues principales. Cada uno lleva exactamente los mismos seis campos.\n\nIssue 1 — <short title>\nPon un título muy corto que identifique el problema. No es todavía la explicación. Piensa: “¿cómo llamaría a este bug en pocas palabras?”\n\nWhat was wrong or weak:\nExplica cómo estaba el sistema antes de tu cambio y por qué era un problema. No empieces diciendo qué hiciste. Primero explica el defecto. Preguntas que te puedes hacer: ¿qué comportamiento incorrecto ocurría?, ¿quién podía verse afectado?, ¿era seguridad, datos, arquitectura o validación?\n\nShape of my improvement:\nAhora sí: explica la forma general de tu solución. No necesitan un recorrido línea por línea del código. Quieren saber qué cambiaste conceptualmente y dónde pusiste la responsabilidad. Pregúntate: ¿qué frontera cambié?, ¿qué módulo quedó responsable?, ¿qué comportamiento pasó de antes a después?\n\nConfidence (1–10):\nElige un número de 1 a 10. No es “qué tan orgulloso estoy”, sino qué tan seguro estoy de que esta solución resuelve ese problema correctamente. Luego tienes que ser capaz de explicar por qué no pusiste más o menos.\n\nWhat would falsify this fix:\nEsta es de las preguntas más importantes. Significa: ¿qué escenario concreto demostraría que mi arreglo está equivocado? Debe ser algo comprobable. No escribas algo genérico como “que falle”. Piensa en una petición, input, usuario, comportamiento o test específico que, si ocurriera, demostraría que tu solución no cumplió su objetivo.\n\nI disagreed with Claude on:\nAquí quieren saber si durante ese issue rechazaste, redujiste o modificaste una sugerencia de IA. Puede ser porque era demasiado compleja, no encajaba con el scope, introducía infraestructura innecesaria o simplemente preferiste otra solución. No inventes un desacuerdo. Si para ese issue no hubo ninguno, el propio template permite decir que no hubo desacuerdo.\n\nAunque el campo diga Claude, si utilizaste otras herramientas de IA durante el reto, lo importante es que seas coherente con tu historial real.\n\nAlternatives I considered and rejected:\nMenciona otras formas que realmente pensaste usar y por qué no las escogiste. Aquí están evaluando juicio. No basta con “había otras opciones”; debes saber qué alternativa era y cuál fue el costo o desventaja que te hizo descartarla.\n\nIssue 2 y Issue 3\nSe responden exactamente con la misma lógica. All order queries should go through here.\r\n *\r\n * - centralized place for query patterns\r\n * - the place to add auditing, caching, tenancy filters\r\n * - the seam for swapping the underlying store\r\n */\r\nexport const ordersDal = {\r\n  listByMerchant(\r\n    merchantId: string,\r\n    opts: { from?: string; to?: string; limit?: number } = {},\r\n  ): OrderRow[] {\r\n    const limit = opts.limit ?? 100;\r\n\r\n    if (opts.from && opts.to) {\r\n      return db\r\n        .prepare(\r\n          SELECT * FROM orders\r\n           WHERE merchant_id = ? AND created_at >= ? AND created_at < ?\r\n           ORDER BY created_at DESC\r\n           LIMIT ?,\r\n        )\r\n        .all(merchantId, opts.from, opts.to, limit) as OrderRow[];\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    return db\r\n      .prepare(\r\n        SELECT * FROM orders\r\n         WHERE merchant_id = ?\r\n         ORDER BY created_at DESC\r\n         LIMIT ?,\r\n      )\r\n      .all(merchantId, limit) as OrderRow[];\r\n  },\r\n\r\n  getById(merchantId: string, id: string): OrderRow | undefined {\r\n    return db\r\n      .prepare(SELECT * FROM orders WHERE merchant_id = ? AND id = ?)\r\n      .get(merchantId, id) as OrderRow | undefined;\r\n  },\r\n\r\n  create(order: Omit<OrderRow, 'created_at'>): OrderRow {\r\n    db.prepare(\r\n      INSERT INTO orders (id, merchant_id, customer_email, total_amount, type, status)\r\n       VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?),\r\n    ).run(\r\n      order.id,\r\n      order.merchant_id,\r\n      order.customer_email,\r\n      order.total_amount,\r\n      order.type,\r\n      order.status,\r\n    );\r\n\r\n    return this.getById(order.merchant_id, order.id)!;\r\n  },\r\n\r\n  /**\r\n   * Sum total_amount over a date range for a merchant.\r\n   * Used by the revenue endpoint.\r\n   */\r\n  sumAmountByMerchant(merchantId: string, from: string, to: string): number {\r\n    const row = db\r\n      .prepare(\r\n        SELECT COALESCE(\r\n           SUM(\r\n             CASE\r\n               WHEN type = 'refund' THEN -total_amount\r\n               ELSE total_amount\r\n             END\r\n           ),\r\n           0\r\n         ) AS total\r\n         FROM orders\r\n         WHERE merchant_id = ? AND created_at >= ? AND created_at < ?,\r\n      )\r\n      .get(merchantId, from, to) as { total: number };\r\n\r\n    return row.total;\r\n  },\r\n\r\n  getSummaryByMerchant(merchantId: string): MerchantSummary {\r\n    const totalOrdersRow = db\r\n      .prepare(SELECT COUNT(*) AS n FROM orders WHERE merchant_id = ?)\r\n      .get(merchantId) as { n: number };\r\n\r\n    const totalCustomersRow = db\r\n      .prepare(\r\n        SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT customer_email) AS n\r\n         FROM orders\r\n         WHERE merchant_id = ?,\r\n      )\r\n      .get(merchantId) as { n: number };\r\n\r\n    const avgOrderRow = db\r\n      .prepare(\r\n        SELECT COALESCE(AVG(total_amount), 0) AS avg\r\n         FROM orders\r\n         WHERE merchant_id = ?,\r\n      )\r\n      .get(merchantId) as { avg: number };\r\n\r\n    return {\r\n      total_orders: totalOrdersRow.n,\r\n      unique_customers: totalCustomersRow.n,\r\n      avg_order_value_cents: Math.round(avgOrderRow.avg),\r\n    };\r\n  },\r\n\r\n  getTopCustomersByMerchant(\r\n    merchantId: string,\r\n    limit: number,\r\n  ): TopCustomer[] {\r\n    return db\r\n      .prepare(\r\n        SELECT\r\n           customer_email,\r\n           COUNT(*) AS order_count,\r\n           SUM(\r\n             CASE\r\n               WHEN type = 'refund' THEN -total_amount\r\n               ELSE total_amount\r\n             END\r\n           ) AS total_spent\r\n         FROM orders\r\n         WHERE merchant_id = ?\r\n         GROUP BY customer_email\r\n         ORDER BY total_spent DESC\r\n         LIMIT ?,\r\n      )\r\n      .all(merchantId, limit) as TopCustomer[];\r\n  },\r\n};\r\n\nMostrar más"

Es mejor que pegarle al servidor cada segundo porque cada fallo espera más antes del siguiente intento.\n\nPero hay un problema si hacemos simplemente:\n\nTypeScript\nawait sleep(100);\nretry();\n\nawait sleep(200);\nretry();\n\ndentro de nuestro proceso.\n\nImagina esto:\n\nWebhook falla\n↓\nesperamos 200ms\n↓\nEC2 / Node se reinicia 💥\n↓\nse perdió el webhook\n\nEntonces eso no garantiza “no perder nada”.\n\nPara realmente ponerlo “en cola hasta que vuelva”, necesitaríamos guardar el intento en algo persistente. Como ya tenemos SQLite, podríamos hacerlo sin meter Redis, Kafka ni nada exagerado:\n\norder.created\n      ↓\nguardar delivery en SQLite\n      ↓\nintentar POST\n      ↓\n200 ✅ → marcar delivered\n\n500 ❌\n      ↓\nattempts = 1\nnext_attempt_at = +100ms\n      ↓\nintentar otra vez\n      ↓\n500 ❌\nnext_attempt_at = +200ms\n      ↓\n...\n\nUna tabla conceptual podría tener:\n\nwebhook_deliveries\n------------------\nid\nsubscription_id\nevent\npayload\nstatus\nattempt_count\nnext_attempt_at\nlast_error\ncreated_at\n\nY eso sí tiene una ventaja fuerte:\n\nNode se cae\n↓\nNode vuelve a levantar\n↓\nla entrega sigue en SQLite\n↓\nse puede volver a intentar ✅\nPero para este challenge\n\nYo no haría retries infinitos hasta que el merchant vuelva, porque ya empieza a crecer bastante el sistema.\n\nHaría algo limitado, por ejemplo:\n\nIntento 1 → inmediato\nIntento 2 → +200 ms\nIntento 3 → +400 ms\nIntento 4 → +800 ms\n↓\nsi sigue fallando\nmarcar failed\n\nEso demuestra que entendemos:\n\nfallos externos,\n\nretries,\n\nexponential backoff,\n\nevitar bombardear al receptor,\n\ny que una orden no debe fallar porque el webhook falle.\n\nY si quisiéramos llevarlo a producción de verdad, ahí sí evolucionaría a cola persistente / outbox.\n\nTu intuición de “no quiero perder el evento y tampoco quiero estar haciendo peticiones todo el tiempo” es exactamente el problema que los retries con backoff intentan resolver.\n\nPara esta prueba, yo elegiría retries acotados con exponential backoff, no una infraestructura completa de colas. Es un mejor equilibrio entre robustez y no sobreingeniería."

El worker encuentra el webhook pendiente\n\nActualmente, en el paso 5 todavía podría intentar enviarlo, porque fue creado antes de que la suscripción se desactivara.\n\nComo decidimos que:\n\nactive = false\n→ ya no quiero recibir nada de esta suscripción\n\nentonces también debemos cancelar esos pendientes.\n\nEn:\n\nsrc/services/webhook-delivery.ts\n\nbusca esta parte:\n\nTypeScript\nconst subscription = webhooksDal.getById(\n  delivery.subscription_id,\n);\n\nif (!subscription) {\n  console.error(\n    `Webhook subscription not found: ${delivery.subscription_id}`,\n  );\n\n  webhookDeliveriesDal.remove(delivery.id);\n  return;\n}\n\ny cámbiala por:\n\nTypeScript\nconst subscription = webhooksDal.getById(\n  delivery.subscription_id,\n);\n\nif (!subscription || subscription.active !== 1) {\n  webhookDeliveriesDal.remove(delivery.id);\n  return;\n}\n\nEn palabras simples:\n\n¿La suscripción ya no existe?\n→ borrar entrega pendiente\n\n¿Está desactivada?\n→ borrar entrega pendiente\n\n¿Sigue activa?\n→ intentar enviarla normalmente\n\nAdemás, para eventos nuevos ya estamos protegidos porque listActiveByMerchantAndEvent() sólo busca:\n\nSQL\nactive = 1\n\nAsí que después de este cambio:\n\nSuscripción activa\n→ recibe eventos ✅\n\nSuscripción desactivada\n→ no recibe eventos nuevos ✅\n→ tampoco recibe pendientes antiguos ✅\n\nHaz ese pequeño cambio y luego:\n\nPowerShell\nnpm test\nnpm run build\n\nSi queda verde, agregamos los tests específicos para desactivación + aislamiento entre merchants."

1),\r\n    MAX_BACKOFF_MS,\r\n  );\r\n}\r\n\r\nasync function attemptWebhookDelivery(\r\n  delivery: WebhookDeliveryRow,\r\n): Promise<void> {\r\n  const subscription = webhooksDal.getById(\r\n    delivery.subscription_id,\r\n  );\r\n\r\n  if (!subscription) {\r\n    console.error(\r\n      Webhook subscription not found: ${delivery.subscription_id},\r\n    );\r\n\r\n    webhookDeliveriesDal.remove(delivery.id);\r\n    return;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  const signature = createHmac(\r\n    'sha256',\r\n    subscription.secret,\r\n  )\r\n    .update(delivery.payload)\r\n    .digest('hex');\r\n\r\n  try {\r\n    const response = await fetch(subscription.url, {\r\n      method: 'POST',\r\n      headers: {\r\n        'Content-Type': 'application/json',\r\n        'X-Webhook-Event': delivery.event,\r\n        'X-Webhook-Signature': signature,\r\n        'X-Webhook-Delivery-Id': delivery.id,\r\n      },\r\n      body: delivery.payload,\r\n      signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000),\r\n    });\r\n\r\n    if (response.ok) {\r\n      webhookDeliveriesDal.remove(delivery.id);\r\n      return;\r\n    }\r\n\r\n    const nextAttemptCount = delivery.attempt_count + 1;\r\n    const delay = retryDelayMs(nextAttemptCount);\r\n\r\n    webhookDeliveriesDal.markFailure(\r\n      delivery.id,\r\n      nextAttemptCount,\r\n      Date.now() + delay,\r\n      HTTP ${response.status},\r\n    );\r\n\r\n    console.error(\r\n      Webhook failed: ${delivery.id} returned ${response.status}.  +\r\n      Retry in ${delay}ms,\r\n    );\r\n  } catch (error) {\r\n    const nextAttemptCount = delivery.attempt_count + 1;\r\n    const delay = retryDelayMs(nextAttemptCount);\r\n\r\n    const message =\r\n      error instanceof Error\r\n        ? error.message\r\n        : String(error);\r\n\r\n    webhookDeliveriesDal.markFailure(\r\n      delivery.id,\r\n      nextAttemptCount,\r\n      Date.now() + delay,\r\n      message,\r\n    );\r\n\r\n    console.error(\r\n      Webhook failed: ${delivery.id}. Retry in ${delay}ms,\r\n      error,\r\n    );\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport async function processDueWebhookDeliveries(): Promise<void> {\r\n  if (processingDeliveries) {\r\n    return;\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  processingDeliveries = true;\r\n\r\n  try {\r\n    const deliveries = webhookDeliveriesDal.listDue(\r\n      Date.now(),\r\n    );\r\n\r\n    for (const delivery of deliveries) {\r\n      await attemptWebhookDelivery(delivery);\r\n    }\r\n  } finally {\r\n    processingDeliveries = false;\r\n  }\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport async function deliverWebhookEvent(\r\n  merchantId: string,\r\n  event: WebhookEvent,\r\n  order: OrderPayload,\r\n): Promise<void> {\r\n  const subscriptions =\r\n    webhooksDal.listActiveByMerchantAndEvent(\r\n      merchantId,\r\n      event,\r\n    );\r\n\r\n  for (const subscription of subscriptions) {\r\n    const payload = JSON.stringify({\r\n      event,\r\n      order,\r\n    });\r\n\r\n    webhookDeliveriesDal.create({\r\n      id: randomUUID(),\r\n      subscription_id: subscription.id,\r\n      event,\r\n      payload,\r\n      next_attempt_at: Date.now(),\r\n    });\r\n  }\r\n\r\n  await processDueWebhookDeliveries();\r\n}\r\n\r\nexport function startWebhookDeliveryWorker(\r\n  intervalMs = 250,\r\n): NodeJS.Timeout {\r\n  const timer = setInterval(() => {\r\n    void processDueWebhookDeliveries().catch((error) => {\r\n      console.error(\r\n        'Webhook delivery worker error:',\r\n        error,\r\n      );\r\n    });\r\n  }, intervalMs);\r\n\r\n  timer.unref();\r\n\r\n  return timer;\r\n}\nMostrar más"

Reemplazaría docs/api.md por esto:\n\nMarkdown\n# API reference\n\nAll API endpoints except `GET /api/health` require the `X-Merchant-Id` header.\n\n## Health\n\n### `GET /api/health`\n\nNo authentication required.\n\nResponse:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"ok\": true\n}\nOrders\nGET /api/orders\n\nReturns orders for the authenticated merchant.\n\nOptional query parameters:\n\nfrom\n\nto\n\nlimit\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"orders\": []\n}\nGET /api/orders/:id\n\nReturns a single order belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nReturns 404 when the order does not exist or belongs to another merchant.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"order\": {\n    \"id\": \"order-id\",\n    \"merchant_id\": \"merchant-id\",\n    \"customer_email\": \"customer@example.com\",\n    \"total_amount\": 5000,\n    \"type\": \"sale\",\n    \"status\": \"completed\",\n    \"created_at\": \"2026-08-14 00:00:00\"\n  }\n}\nPOST /api/orders\n\nCreates an order for the authenticated merchant.\n\nBody:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"customer_email\": \"customer@example.com\",\n  \"total_amount\": 5000,\n  \"type\": \"sale\"\n}\n\nRules:\n\ncustomer_email must be a non-empty string.\n\ntotal_amount must be a positive integer.\n\ntype may be sale or refund.\n\ntype defaults to sale when omitted.\n\nNew orders are created with status completed.\n\nReturns 400 with { \"error\": \"invalid_body\" } when validation fails.\n\nCreating a sale emits order.created.\nCreating a refund emits order.refunded.\n\nPATCH /api/orders/:id/status\n\nUpdates the status of an order belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nBody:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"status\": \"cancelled\"\n}\n\nstatus must be a non-empty string.\n\nReturns:\n\n400 for an invalid body.\n\n404 when the order does not exist or belongs to another merchant.\n\nWhen the status changes, the API emits an order.status_changed webhook event.\n\nRevenue\nGET /api/revenue?from=...&to=...\n\nReturns revenue for the authenticated merchant in the requested date range.\n\nBoth from and to are required.\n\nRefund amounts are subtracted from revenue.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"merchant_id\": \"merchant-id\",\n  \"from\": \"2026-08-01\",\n  \"to\": \"2026-09-01\",\n  \"revenue_cents\": 12500,\n  \"revenue\": 125\n}\n\nReturns 400 with missing_date_range when from or to is missing.\n\nMetrics\nGET /api/metrics/summary\n\nReturns dashboard summary metrics for the authenticated merchant.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"merchant_id\": \"merchant-id\",\n  \"total_orders\": 10,\n  \"unique_customers\": 7,\n  \"avg_order_value_cents\": 4200\n}\nGET /api/metrics/top-customers\n\nReturns customers ordered by total spent.\n\nOptional query parameter:\n\nlimit — defaults to 5.\n\nRefunds are subtracted from each customer's total_spent.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"customers\": [\n    {\n      \"customer_email\": \"customer@example.com\",\n      \"order_count\": 3,\n      \"total_spent\": 12000\n    }\n  ]\n}\nWebhooks\n\nSupported events:\n\norder.created\n\norder.refunded\n\norder.status_changed\n\nPOST /api/webhooks\n\nRegisters an HTTPS webhook endpoint for one or more supported events.\n\nBody:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"url\": \"https://example.com/webhooks\",\n  \"events\": [\n    \"order.created\",\n    \"order.refunded\"\n  ]\n}\n\nThe URL must use HTTPS and at least one valid event must be provided.\nDuplicate events in the same request are ignored.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"webhook\": {\n    \"url\": \"https://example.com/webhooks\",\n    \"events\": [\n      \"order.created\",\n      \"order.refunded\"\n    ],\n    \"secret\": \"generated-secret\",\n    \"subscription_ids\": [\n      \"subscription-id-1\",\n      \"subscription-id-2\"\n    ]\n  }\n}\n\nThe generated secret is returned when the webhook is registered and is not included when subscriptions are later listed.\n\nGET /api/webhooks\n\nLists webhook subscriptions belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nSecrets are not returned.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"webhooks\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"subscription-id\",\n      \"url\": \"https://example.com/webhooks\",\n      \"event\": \"order.created\",\n      \"active\": true,\n      \"created_at\": \"2026-08-14 00:00:00\"\n    }\n  ]\n}\nDELETE /api/webhooks/:id\n\nDeactivates a webhook subscription belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nThe subscription is retained but marked inactive.\n\nReturns:\n\n204 when successfully deactivated.\n\n404 when the subscription does not exist, is already inactive, or belongs to another merchant.\n\nWebhook delivery\n\nWebhook requests use POST with a JSON body:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"event\": \"order.created\",\n  \"order\": {\n    \"id\": \"order-id\",\n    \"merchant_id\": \"merchant-id\",\n    \"customer_email\": \"customer@example.com\",\n    \"total_amount\": 5000,\n    \"type\": \"sale\",\n    \"status\": \"completed\",\n    \"created_at\": \"2026-08-14 00:00:00\"\n  }\n}\n\nWebhook requests include:\n\nContent-Type: application/json\n\nX-Webhook-Event\n\nX-Webhook-Signature\n\nX-Webhook-Delivery-Id\n\nX-Webhook-Signature is an HMAC-SHA256 signature of the exact JSON request body using the subscription secret.\n\nFailed deliveries are persisted and retried using exponential backoff. The retry delay starts at 100 ms and is capped at 60 seconds.\n\nSuccessful deliveries are removed from the pending delivery queue. Pending deliveries are also removed if their subscription is deactivated.\n\n### Por qué así\n\nNo pondría aquí SSRF, outbox, múltiples workers, dead-letter queue, etc. Eso no es **API reference**; son decisiones/limitaciones arquitectónicas y van mejor en `docs/architecture.md` o en tus entregables humanos cuando corresponda.\n\nTambién evitaría afirmar cosas que el código no valida. Por ejemplo, **no puse que `from` y `to` sean fechas válidas**, porque el código sólo comprueba que existan. Tampoco puse una lista oficial de estados permitidos, porque actualmente `status` acepta cualquier string no vacío.\n\nEso hace que la documentación sea fuerte por una razón sencilla: **describe lo que el sistema realmente hace, no lo que nos gustaría que hiciera**.\n\nSi reemplazas `docs/api.md` con esto, el siguiente archivo sería `docs/architecture.md`."

\"subscription-id-1\",\n      \"subscription-id-2\"\n    ]\n  }\n}\n\nA secret is generated when the webhook is registered.\n\nThe secret is returned during registration so the receiver can validate webhook signatures.\n\nThe secret is not included when subscriptions are later listed.\n\nInternally, one subscription record is stored for each selected event.\n\nGET /api/webhooks\n\nLists webhook subscriptions belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nSecrets are not returned.\n\nResponse:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"webhooks\": [\n    {\n      \"id\": \"subscription-id\",\n      \"url\": subscriptions may appear in the response.\n\nThe active field indicates whether the subscription is currently enabled.\n\nDELETE /api/webhooks/:id\n\nDeactivates a webhook subscription belonging to the authenticated merchant.\n\nThe subscription is not physically deleted.\n\nInstead, it is marked inactive so its record can be retained.\n\nReturns:\n\n204 No Content\n\nwhen successfully deactivated.\n\nReturns 404 when:\n\nthe subscription does not exist;\n\nit is already inactive;\n\nor it belongs to another merchant.\n\nA merchant cannot deactivate subscriptions belonging to another merchant.\n\nWebhook delivery\n\nWebhook requests are sent using HTTP POST.\n\nThe request body contains the event name and the related order.\n\nExample:\n\nJSON\n{\n  \"event\": \"order.created\",\n  \"order\": {\n    \"id\": \"order-id\",\n    \"merchant_id\": \"merchant-id\",\n    \"customer_email\": \"customer@example.com\",\n    \"total_amount\": 5000,\n    \"type\": \"sale\",\n    \"status\": \"completed\",\n    \"created_at\": \"2026-08-14 00:00:00\"\n  }\n}\n\nWebhook requests include the following headers:\n\nContent-Type: application/json\nX-Webhook-Event: order.created\nX-Webhook-Signature: <signature>\nX-Webhook-Delivery-Id: <delivery-id>\nSignature verification\n\nX-Webhook-Signature contains an HMAC-SHA256 signature.\n\nThe signature is calculated using:\n\nthe exact JSON request body;\n\nand the secret generated when the webhook was registered.\n\nConceptually:\n\nHMAC-SHA256(subscription_secret, request_body)\n\nThe receiver can compute the same signature and compare it with the value received in X-Webhook-Signature.\n\nDelivery persistence and retries\n\nWebhook deliveries are persisted before delivery is attempted.\n\nA pending delivery remains stored until:\n\ndelivery succeeds;\n\nor its webhook subscription is deactivated.\n\nWhen an endpoint returns a non-successful HTTP response or cannot be reached, the delivery is retained and scheduled for retry.\n\nRetries use exponential backoff.\n\nThe delay starts at approximately:\n\n100 ms\n200 ms\n400 ms\n800 ms\n1600 ms\n...\n\nThe retry delay is capped at:\n\n60 seconds\n\nAfter reaching the cap, failed deliveries continue to be retried with a maximum delay of 60 seconds between attempts.\n\nWhen a delivery succeeds, its pending delivery record is removed.\n\nIf a subscription is deactivated while it still has a pending delivery, that pending delivery is removed and is not sent again.\n\nMerchant isolation\n\nAPI access is scoped using the X-Merchant-Id header.\n\nOrders and webhook subscriptions are restricted to the authenticated merchant.\n\nA merchant cannot:\n\nretrieve another merchant's order;\n\nupdate another merchant's order status;\n\ndeactivate another merchant's webhook subscription.\n\nRequests attempting to access another merchant's resources behave as if the resource were unavailable to that merchant."

## What Claude (or your AI tool) got wrong

> 

### Example 1

- **The prompt:** I asked what should happen when a webhook receiver was down and suggested keeping the failed delivery in a queue with exponential backoff instead of losing it.
- **What ChatGPT returned:** ChatGPT initially recommended keeping the Webhooks feature small and explicitly suggested not building queues or workers. The first implementation only attempted the delivery and logged the failure without guaranteeing that the event would survive a process restart.
- **What was wrong with it** (technically, not just stylistically): A failed webhook could be lost. If the external receiver was unavailable, or if Node restarted before the event could be retried, there was no durable copy of the delivery.
- **How I caught it** (what tipped you off — code reading, test failure, behavior in the browser, prior experience): While reviewing the failure behavior, I questioned what would happen if the receiver stayed offline. I realized that logging the error was not enough because I wanted the event to remain available until the receiver recovered.
- **What I did instead** (and where to see it in the diff, if applicable): I changed the design to persist webhook deliveries in SQLite before sending them. Failed deliveries remain stored and are retried by a worker using exponential backoff. Successful deliveries are removed from the table. This can be seen in commit `17e9a5f`.

### Example 2

- **The prompt:** During the final Webhooks review, I asked AI to audit the implementation and compare what the code was actually sending with the tests and types.
- **What ChatGPT returned:** The earlier implementation and test model for `OrderPayload` did not include `merchant_id`, even though the real order object sent by the application already contained it.
- **What was wrong with it:** The external webhook contract and the TypeScript/test contract were inconsistent. The real receiver could get `merchant_id`, but the declared payload type and automated delivery tests did not represent that field.
- **How I caught it:** During the final audit I compared the real payload received by my external test server with the TypeScript payload definition and the automated tests. The real delivery contained `merchant_id`, while the test objects did not.
- **What I did instead:** I made `merchant_id` explicit in the Webhook order payload and updated the delivery tests so each test order carries the correct merchant context. This correction can be seen in commit `29bf0c2`.

### Example 3

- **The prompt:** Near the end of the challenge I asked AI to perform another review of the implementation and documentation before I stopped changing the code.
- **What ChatGPT returned:** Earlier, the recommendation was to stop expanding the quality fixes and avoid opening more validation work. A later AI review tested the `limit` query parameter and found that the assumption that this could be left alone was wrong.
- **What was wrong with it:** `Number(req.query.limit)` allowed invalid external input to reach SQLite. `limit=abc` became `NaN` and could produce a 500 error, while a negative limit could cause unintended SQLite behavior instead of being rejected by the API.
- **How I caught it:** The issue was reproduced with real requests during the final review. This showed that it was not only a theoretical concern: invalid client input was reaching the database and producing incorrect API behavior.
- **What I did instead:** I changed the previous decision and added a small shared query validation helper. Invalid limits are now rejected with HTTP 400 before reaching the DAL/SQLite, and regression tests cover invalid strings, negative values, and valid limits. This can be seen in commit `c447836`.
